“See ’ere,” the man declared, “I’ve been doing this business for long enough to know all about ’ow it’s done. I’ve a good boy, if you must know. You give ’im a penny or two a time if you’re nervous, and you needn’t be afryde but you’ll get all there’s for you.”

===Chinese===

「这我还不能放心。把事情托付给你之前，我总得知道你会派个什么样的送信人。」

「你听着，」那汉子声明道，「这行当我干得够久，个中门道俺全知道。你非要问的话，俺有个不赖的孩子。你要是不放心，一回赏他一两个便士，该你收的口信保险一条不少。」

===Original===

French laid five shillings on the counter.

“Right,” he said. “There’s for the first five messages. Send to Mr. James Hurley, care of Mr. William Wright, tobacconist, corner of Bedford Place and Ivy Street.”

“I know it,” declared Ganope, pocketing the money.

===Chinese===

弗伦奇把五枚先令摆上柜台。

「好，」他说。「这是头五条口信的钱。收件人詹姆斯·赫尔利先生，由烟贩威廉·赖特先生转交，铺子在贝德福德坊与常春藤街的拐角上。」

「那地方俺知道。」加诺普说着，把钱揣进了兜。

===Original===

Mr. William Wright was a distant connection of Mrs. French’s and French knew that he would help him in the matter. He nodded to Ganope and walked across to Ivy Street.

“Hullo, Joe! Some time since we’ve seen you here,” was Mr. Wright’s greeting. “Come in behind and let’s hear the news.”

===Chinese===

威廉·赖特先生是弗伦奇太太的一位远亲，弗伦奇知道，这件事他定会帮忙。他朝加诺普点点头，步行到常春藤街去。

「哟，乔！可有些日子没见你上这儿来了。」赖特先生这样招呼。「到后头来，听听新鲜事儿。」

===Original===

“I want you to do me a good turn, William,” French answered. “There’s a boy I want to get hold of and I’ve fixed it that he’ll come here asking for Mr. Hurley. Will you put him on to me when he turns up? That’s all.”

“Surely, Joe,” and Mr. Wright turned the conversation to more intimate matters.

===Chinese===

「威廉，我要请你帮个忙。」弗伦奇答道。「有个孩子我想找他，我已经安排好了，他会到这儿来讨见赫尔利先生。他来了，你就把他引到我这儿来。就这么多。」

「没问题，乔。」赖特先生说着，把话头转到了家常私事上。

===Original===

“Just let me use your phone,” French asked, presently. “Something I forgot.”

“Surely, Joe.”

Going into the little office at the back of the shop, French rang up Ganope.

===Chinese===

「让我用用你的电话。」过了一会儿，弗伦奇开口。「忘了桩事。」

「没问题，乔。」

弗伦奇走进铺子后头的小写字间，给加诺普挂了电话。

===Original===

“Message for Hurley,” he explained in falsetto tones. “Mr. James Hurley.”

“Right,” came from the other end.

“ ‘Cargo will be in at ten-fifteen tomorrow.’ That’s all. Repeat, please.”

===Chinese===

「有口信给赫尔利。」他捏着假嗓解释道。「詹姆斯·赫尔利先生。」

「好嘞。」那头答道。

「『货明早十点一刻到。』完了。请复述一遍。」

===Original===

Mr. Ganope repeated slowly, evidently as he wrote, and French settled down with his pipe to await the advent of the messenger.

In less than half an hour a sharp, foxy-looking boy turned up and Mr. Wright sent him into the sitting-room to French.

===Chinese===

加诺普慢慢复述着，显然是一边写一边念，弗伦奇装上烟斗坐定，专等那送信的上门。

不到半个钟头，一个尖头尖脑、狐狸似的男孩来了，赖特先生把他领进起居室去见弗伦奇。

===Original===

“Hullo, sonny! You from Ganope’s?”

“Huh-huh,” said the boy. “Name of Hurley?”

“That’s right. You have a message for me?”

“Huh-huh.” He slowly produced an envelope, watching French expectantly.

===Chinese===

「嘿，小家伙！你是加诺普铺子里来的？」

「嗯呐。」男孩说。「找赫尔利先生？」

「不错。带了口信给我？」

「嗯呐。」他慢吞吞掏出一个信封，眼巴巴地望着弗伦奇。

===Original===

French produced sixpence.

“There you are, sonny. Wait half a sec till I read this. There may be an answer.”

===Chinese===

弗伦奇掏出六便士。

「给你，小家伙。稍等片刻，让我看看这个。说不定要回话。」

===Original===

He tore open the envelope and glanced at its contents.

“No, there’s nothing,” he went on. “You’re kept busy, I’m sure?”

“Huh-huh.”

“And maybe you don’t get too much money for it, either?”

===Chinese===

他撕开信封，扫了一眼里头的内容。

「不用，没什么回话。」他接着说。「你这活儿挺忙的吧，我猜？」

「嗯呐。」

「挣的钱恐怕也不多吧？」

===Original===

The boy indicated that this was a true summary of his case.

“Well, how would half a crown for a little job for me suit you?”

The gleam in the boy’s eyes was sufficient answer.

===Chinese===

男孩表示，这话说的正是他的实情。

「那么，替我办件小事，挣半个克朗，你觉得怎么样？」

男孩眼睛里放出的光，就是再充分不过的回答。

===Original===

“It’s just a little bit of information between you and me. No one else would know anything about it. It wouldn’t take you two minutes to give it to me. Are you on?”

“Ain’t I, gov’nor? You just try me.”

===Chinese===

「就是一点小小的消息，天知地知，你知我知。别人谁也不会知道。说出来不消你两分钟。干不干？」

「怎么不干，老板？您只管试俺。」

===Original===

French took some money out of his pocket and slowly counted out two single shillings and a sixpence. These he laid on the table in a little pile.

“Tell me, sonny,” he said. “You had to take a message out this morning shortly after eleven?”

===Chinese===

弗伦奇从口袋里掏出些钱，慢慢数出两枚单先令和一枚六便士，在桌上码成小小的一摞。

「跟我说说，小家伙。」他说。「今天早上十一点刚过，你是不是送出去一条口信？」

===Original===

“Huh-huh.”

“Where did you take it to?”

“You won’t tell the old man?”

“I’ll not, sonny. I give you my word.”

===Chinese===

「嗯呐。」

「送到哪儿去了？」

「您不会告到那老头儿那儿吧？」

「不会的，小家伙。我向你担保。」

===Original===

“Name o’ Pyke, seventeen Kepple Street.”

===Chinese===

「一个姓派克的，凯普尔街十七号。」

===Original===

In spite of his training as an officer of the Yard, French started. Pyke! He remained for a moment lost in thought. Pyke! Could this be the solution at last? Could Mrs. Berlyn have transferred her somewhat facile affections to Jefferson Pyke? Could these two be guilty of the murder of both Stanley and Berlyn?

===Chinese===

弗伦奇虽是苏格兰场的老公事，也不禁吃了一惊。派克！他怔了一会儿，陷入沉思。派克！难道谜底终于露头了？伯林太太那份未免轻浮易动的情愫，会不会已经转到了杰斐逊·派克身上？这两个人，会不会就是谋害斯坦利和伯林两人的凶手？

===Original===

Here was a promising idea! In the first place, it was the solution to the dilemma which had so greatly puzzled him. The crime had been committed to enable the murderer to live with Phyllis in good social standing. Therefore, the murderer could not have disappeared. Therefore, it could not have been either Berlyn or Pyke. There had been French’s problem.

===Chinese===

这可是个有指望的思路！首先，它解开了那个让他大伤脑筋的两难困局。作案的目的，是让凶手能同菲莉斯名正言顺地共同生活。因此，凶手不可能消失无踪。因此，凶手就不可能是伯林或派克。弗伦奇的难题正在这里。

===Original===

But if Jefferson Pyke and Phyllis had been accomplices, all these difficulties vanished. Berlyn and Pyke had disappeared because they were dead. The murderer had survived to enjoy the fruits of his crime. All the facts seemed to be met.

===Chinese===

可倘若杰斐逊·派克同菲莉斯是同谋，这些困难便一齐烟消云散。伯林和派克之所以失踪，是因为他们死了。凶手活了下来，享用罪恶的果实。所有的事实似乎都对上了。

===Original===

In itself, also, this new theory was likely enough. Jefferson and Phyllis had been playmates and an old attachment might easily have flamed up anew on their meeting at Ashburton. If so, there was the motive for Berlyn’s murder. Stanley Pyke might also have been in the way. Possibly Phyllis was so far entangled with him that to break loose would have turned him into a dangerous enemy. Possibly the accomplices feared that he might guess their crime. Under the circumstances it was easy to see that their only safe scheme might well have been to remove both men.

===Chinese===

单就这个新推断本身而论，也相当站得住。杰斐逊和菲莉斯本是青梅竹马的玩伴，在阿什伯顿重逢之后，旧情复燃是很容易的事。倘真如此，谋杀伯林的动机便有了。斯坦利·派克也可能碍了他们的事。也许菲莉斯同他纠缠已深，一旦抽身，就会把他变成一个危险的仇敌。也许这对同谋怕他猜出他们的罪行。在这种种情形下，不难明白，他们唯一稳妥的计策，多半就是把两个男人一并除掉。

===Original===

The details also worked in. Phyllis could have obtained the information about the works necessary for the disposal of the body. Jefferson was of the size and build of the man who had called for the lorry and crate at Swansea, and though his colouring was different, this could have been altered artificially. He could be biding his time until he was sure the affair had blown over to take Phyllis out to his *estancia* in the Argentine.

===Chinese===

种种细节也吻合。菲莉斯有可能弄到处置尸体所需工序的情报。杰斐逊的身量体格，同斯旺西提取卡车和板条箱的那人相仿；虽说肤色不同，那是可以人为改变的。他大可以蛰伏待机，等确信风波已经平息，再把菲莉斯接出去，到他在阿根廷的大牧场（*estancia*）去。

===Original===

Another point occurred to French. Alfred Beer had stated, no doubt in all good faith, that the conversation he had overheard in the Berlyn’s shrubbery was between Mrs. Berlyn and Stanley Pyke, and French had naturally assumed that the “ ’e” referred to was Berlyn. But were he and Beer correct? Might the scene not equally well have been between the lady and Jefferson and might “ ’e” not have been Stanley? French decided to look up his notes of the matter at the earliest opportunity.

===Chinese===

弗伦奇又想起一点。阿尔弗雷德·比尔说过——无疑完全是出于好意——他在伯林家灌木林子里偷听到的那番话，是在伯林太太和斯坦利·派克之间，弗伦奇便自然而然地认定，话里说的那个「他」指的是伯林。可他和比尔认定得对吗？那一幕难道就不可能同样是这位太太和杰斐逊之间的事？那个「他」难道就不可能是斯坦利？弗伦奇决定尽早翻出他关于此事的记录查上一查。

===Original===

He had little doubt that at long last he was on to the truth. Jubilantly he handed over the half-crown to Ganope’s boy and dismissed him with the assurance that he would never hear of the matter again.

===Chinese===

他几乎毫不怀疑，自己总算摸着真相的门径了。他喜滋滋地把半克朗付给加诺普的孩子，一面担保这事往后绝不会再有人提起，一面打发他走了。

===Original===

As to his next step there could be no question. He walked quickly to Kepple Street and asked if Mr. Jefferson Pyke was at home.

Pyke was out, but was expected shortly. Hugging himself for his luck, French said that if he might, he would like to wait for Mr. Pyke. The landlady remembered his previous visit and made no difficulty about showing him up to her lodger’s sitting room.

===Chinese===

下一步怎么办，毫无疑义。他快步走到凯普尔街，打听杰斐逊·派克先生在不在家。

派克出去了，不过眼看就该回来。弗伦奇暗自庆幸手气好，说要是方便，他愿意等等派克先生。房东太太记得他上回来过，毫无难色地把他领上房客的起居室去。

===Original===

Before the door closed behind her French saw that his lucky star was still in the ascendant. There on the chimneypiece stood a note addressed “Mr. Jefferson Pyke” in the same handwriting as that for “Mr. James Hurley.”

===Chinese===

房东太太的门还没在身后带上，弗伦奇就看出自己的福星仍在高照。壁炉台上正立着一封信，收信人写的是「杰斐逊·派克先生」，笔迹同「詹姆斯·赫尔利先生」那封一模一样。

===Original===

French carried an old razor blade in his pocket and in less than a minute the envelope was open. The note read:

“Danger. Meet me tonight at old time and place.”

===Chinese===

弗伦奇口袋里随身带着一片旧剃刀片，不消一分钟，信封就开了。便条上写的是：

「有危险。今晚老时间老地方见。」

===Original===

French swore softly in high delight. He had them now! Here was convincing proof of their guilt.

But it was insufficient to bring into court. He must get something more definite. With skilful fingers he reclosed the envelope and put it back where he had found it. Then he settled down to wait for Pyke.

===Chinese===

弗伦奇喜不自胜，低低咒骂了一声。这下他逮住他们了！这便是他们罪行的铁证。

可这还不够送上法庭的分量。他必须拿到更确凿的东西。他手指灵巧地把信封重新封好，放回原处，然后坐定下来等派克。

===Original===

In less than ten minutes the man appeared. French, smiling his pleasant smile, greeted him apologetically.

“Sorry to trouble you again, Mr. Pyke, but I want to ask you for a little further help in this Ashburton affair. I have made a discovery, but first I must ask you to keep what I have to say to yourself.”

===Chinese===

不到十分钟，那人回来了。弗伦奇堆起他那副讨人喜欢的笑，赔着不是迎了上去。

「派克先生，抱歉又来打扰您。这桩阿什伯顿的案子，我还想请您再帮一点忙。我有了一个发现，不过首先得请您答应，我接下来说的话，您绝不外传。」

===Original===

Pyke nodded.

“Of course, Mr. French. Sit down, won’t you, and tell me what I can do for you.”

===Chinese===

派克点了点头。

「当然可以，弗伦奇先生。请坐，说说有什么要我效劳的。」

===Original===

“All I want is a little information,” French declared, taking the proffered chair. “I may tell you between ourselves that certain facts suggest that Colonel Domlio may have been involved. Can you tell me anything that might help me to a decision, anything that your cousin told you or that Mr. Berlyn may have said in your presence?”

===Chinese===

「我只要一点情况。」弗伦奇一面在让过来的椅子上落座，一面说。「咱们私下说说，某些事实显示，多姆利奥上校可能牵涉在内。您能不能告诉我些有助于我下判断的东西——您堂兄跟您说过什么，或者伯林先生当着您的面讲过什么？」

===Original===

Pyke shook his head.

“Colonel Domlio?” he repeated. “Why, no! I never thought of such a thing, and neither Stanley nor Berlyn ever said anything to suggest it.”

===Chinese===

派克摇了摇头。

「多姆利奥上校？」他重复了一遍。「哎呀，没有的事！这样的念头我从来有过，斯坦利和伯林也从没说过一句引人这样想的话。」

===Original===

French continued to question him long enough to convince him that this was really the business on which he had called. Then he tried to discount the effect which Mrs. Berlyn’s note would have when at last the man had an opportunity to read it.

===Chinese===

弗伦奇又盘问了他一阵，问得足以叫他相信，自己登门为的确实是这桩事。接着他设法预先冲淡伯林太太那封便条将起的作用——这人早晚有机会读到它。

===Original===

“I’ve been to see Mrs. Berlyn on this matter,” he explained. “I’m afraid I was rather rude to her, but I just had to frighten her in order to satisfy myself as to whether or not she suspected the colonel. But at least I apologised afterward. I think she forgave me.”

“And did she suspect Domlio?”

“No, I’m sure she did not. And that counts in the colonel’s favour, for she knew him pretty well. Aren’t you thankful you’re not a detective, Mr. Pyke?”

===Chinese===

「为这事我去看过伯林太太。」他解释道。「恐怕我对她相当失礼，可我非得吓一吓她，好弄清她到底疑不疑心上校。不过后来我总归赔了不是。我想她已经原谅我了。」

「那她疑心过多姆利奥吗？」

「没有，我敢肯定她没有。这对上校是有利的，因为她同他相知颇深。派克先生，您不庆幸自己不是干侦探的吗？」

===Original===

They chatted easily for some moments and then French thinking that some information about the other’s former movements might be useful, turned the conversation to travel.

===Chinese===

两人闲聊了一阵，随后弗伦奇心想，关于对方先前行踪的情况或许有用，便把话头引到了旅行上。

===Original===

“You had a pleasant trip to the south of France with your late cousin, I understand?” he remarked. “I wonder if you’d tell me something about it? I’ve just done enough traveling myself to whet my appetite for more, and the idea of the south of France absolutely fascinates me. What part did you go to?”

===Chinese===

「我听说，您同您那位已故的堂兄去法国南部做过一次愉快的旅行？」他说道。「能不能给我讲讲？我自己旅行过的那一点点，恰好吊起了我更大的胃口，法国南部这地方简直叫我着迷。你们去了哪一带？」

===Original===

“The Riviera and Provence,” Pyke answered, with a subtle change of manner. Up to the present he had been polite; now he was interested. “I can tell you, Inspector, that if you’re fond of travelling you should try those districts. You’d enjoy every moment of it.”

“It’s not doubt of that which keeps me away. Money and time are my trouble. Did you get as far as Italy?”

===Chinese===

「里维埃拉和普罗旺斯。」派克答道，态度起了微妙的变化。在这之前，他一直客气应酬；此刻他来了兴趣。「探长，我跟您说，您要是喜欢旅行，就该去那几个地方走走。保您时时刻刻都乐在其中。」

「拦着我不去的可不是这份疑虑。我的难处是钱和时间。你们一直到了意大利吗？」

===Original===

“Yes. I’ll tell you our itinerary if you care to hear it. We went round to Marseilles by long sea. A jolly sail, that. Then﻿—”

“Good weather?” French interposed. He wanted plenty of detail so that he could check the statements up.

===Chinese===

「到了。您要愿意听，我就把我们的路线讲给您。我们是坐海船绕道去的马赛。那一路航行真带劲。后来——」

「天气好不好？」弗伦奇插嘴道。他要的是细节越多越好，好把这些话一一查证。

===Original===

“It was dirty when we left Birkenhead and choppy in the Irish Sea. But it quieted down as we got across the Bay, and from St. Vincent to Gata the sea was like a mirror﻿—an absolute flat calm without a ripple showing. Glorious! Then after Gata we ran into fog, which wasn’t so nice. But we got into Marseilles on time.”

===Chinese===

「从伯肯黑德开船时天色很坏，爱尔兰海里浪头颠簸。可过了比斯开湾便平息下来，从圣文森特到加塔，海面平得像镜子——绝对的风平浪静，连一丝波纹都见不着。痛快极了！过了加塔又遇上雾，那就不那么美了。不过我们还是准点进了马赛。」

===Original===

“Birkenhead? That’s the Bibby Line, isn’t it?”

“Yes, we went on the *Flintshire*. Fine boat of 16,000 tons.”

“I’ve heard those boats well spoken of. I envy you, Mr. Pyke. Then from Marseilles?”

===Chinese===

「伯肯黑德？那是比比轮船公司的船，对不对？」

「对，我们坐的是『弗林特郡号』（*Flintshire*）。一条 16,000 吨的好船。」

「那种船我听人夸过。派克先生，我真羡慕您。那么从马赛呢？」

===Original===

“From Marseilles we went straight through to San Remo. We spent three or four days there, then worked back to Grasse; that’s a small town where they make perfume, between Nice and Cannes, but a bit inland. I’m considering going into the flower trade and I wanted to see the gardens. A wonderful sight all that country must be with flowers in the season!”

===Chinese===

「从马赛我们一径到了圣雷莫，在那儿盘桓了三四天，然后折回去到格拉斯——那是介乎尼斯和戛纳之间、稍靠内陆的一座小镇，人家在那儿造香水。我正盘算着做花卉生意，想去看看那些园子。到了花季，那一带该是何等好看的景致！」

===Original===

“I’ve read about it,” French assured him. “It’s one of the places I’ve got on my list. Flowers and pretty girls, eh?”

“That’s right. We had a dandy specimen to show us round the perfume factory.”

===Chinese===

「这个我读到过。」弗伦奇应道。「那是我列在单子上的地方之一。鲜花配美人，嗯？」

「可不是。领我们参观香水工厂的，就是位顶出色的样品。」

===Original===

“I guess I’ll see that factory,” French declared. “Are there good hotels in a small place like that?”

“We stayed at the Metropole and it was quite all right. Then we worked slowly up through Provence, staying a night in different towns﻿—Marseilles, Nîmes, Arles, Avignon, and so on to Paris. I’ve got all mixed about the places, we saw so many. An interesting country that, Inspector! There are buildings standing there for sixteen hundred years and more. Wonderful! First chance you get you should go down and see for yourself. But don’t do it as we did.”

===Chinese===

「那家工厂我多半也得去看看。」弗伦奇宣称。「那么小的地方，有好旅馆吗？」

「我们住的是大都会饭店（Metropole），相当不错。然后我们取道普罗旺斯缓缓北上，在各处城镇各宿一夜——马赛、尼姆、阿尔勒、阿维尼翁，如此等等，直到巴黎。地方见得太多，我都记混了。探长，那真是个有意思的国家！有些房子在那里立了一千六百多年。了不起！您一得空就该亲自去走走。不过别照我们那样走法。」

===Original===

“How do you mean?”

“Don’t go from place to place, staying in each for a night. Stay at a centre. Avignon is a first rate one. There are day charabanc trips that let you see all these places and you don’t have the nuisance of packing and going to a fresh hotel every day.”

===Chinese===

「怎么讲？」

「别一个地方一个地方地赶，每个地方只住一夜。拣一个中心住下来，阿维尼翁就是头等的好据点。有当日往返的观光大客车，把这些地方都看得到，也省得天天收拾行李、另换一家旅馆的麻烦。」

===Original===

“That’s a point, certainly. Thanks for the tip. Where do you recommend staying in Paris?”

If Pyke recognised that French was merely pumping him he gave no sign, but replied, readily:

===Chinese===

「这话确实在理。多谢指点。巴黎住哪儿，您有推荐没有？」

派克即便看出弗伦奇是在套他的话，也不露分毫，只痛痛快快地答道：

===Original===

“We stayed at the Regina and found it quite good. But we just broke the journey for a night. Next day we came on here.”

“To these rooms?”

“No. I hadn’t taken them then. We stayed a night at the Houston. That’s the night I had such a joke on Stanley.”

===Chinese===

「我们住的是雷吉娜饭店（Regina），觉得相当不错。不过我们只是中途歇了一夜，第二天就到这儿了。」

「直接到这几间屋子里来了？」

「没有。那时这几间还没租下呢。我们在休斯敦饭店（Houston）过了一夜。就是在那一晚，我好好地耍笑了斯坦利一场。」

===Original===

“That sounds interesting.”

“I don’t know how he did it, but while he was dressing he knocked the basin off the stand and it broke and soaked his shoes and deluged the room. You know they have old-fashioned separate basins there, not running water as you get abroad. I imagine he must have set the basin down on a corner of the towel, and when he picked up the towel it twitched the basin off. The wretched thing must have been cracked, for it went into two pieces. But they charged him for it, all right.”

===Chinese===

「这倒有几分好听的。」

「我也闹不清他是怎么弄的，穿衣裳的时候，他把脸盆从盆架上碰了下去，摔破了，水浸了他的鞋，还把屋子淹了一片。您知道，那边的旅馆还是老式的单独脸盆，不像国外那样有自来水。我猜想，他准是把盆子搁在了毛巾的一角上，等他拿起毛巾，就把盆子带翻了。那倒霉玩意儿想必本来就带着裂纹，一摔就成了两半。不过人家可是照价叫他赔了钱的。」

===Original===

“Rough luck, Mr. Pyke. And after that you both thought one night enough to stay?”

Pyke laughed.

“I wanted rooms,” he explained. “Next day I found these and moved in.”

===Chinese===

「真够倒霉的，派克先生。在那以后，你们俩就觉得住一夜够了吗？」

派克笑了。

「我是要找住处。」他解释道。「第二天我找到了这几间，就搬了进来。」

===Original===

“I suppose your late cousin went back to Ashburton at the same time?”

“Not quite directly, I think. He went to see some people near Bath. I believe he said for two days only, but I’m not sure.”

===Chinese===

「您那位已故的堂兄，想必也是那时候回阿什伯顿去的吧？」

「我想没有直接回去。他去巴斯附近看了几家朋友。我记得他说只待两天，不过我拿不准。」

===Original===

“Do you soon return to the Argentine, Mr. Pyke?”

“I’ve not made up my mind. I like this country and I’m half thinking of settling down here and growing flowers for the London market. I’m sick of ranching, anyway, and I’ve instructed my solicitors to begin negotiations for the sale of my property. Of course I’ll have to go out to see to that, but I think I’ll come back very soon.”

===Chinese===

「派克先生，您不久要回阿根廷去吗？」

「还没拿定主意。我喜欢这个国家，颇有心思在这儿落脚，种花供给伦敦的市场。反正牧场我是经营腻了，已经吩咐我的律师们着手谈判出售产业的事。当然，那趟我总得亲自回去料理，不过我想我很快就会回来。」

===Original===

They continued chatting about the Roman remains in Provence until at last French looked at his watch and said he must go.

He was jubilant as to his progress. He believed that he had accounted for his presence and that Pyke suspected nothing. He had also obtained enough information to check practically the whole of the man’s movements from his departure with Stanley for his holiday down to the present time. The end of the affair was in sight. And that evening, he promised himself, should see him a step farther on his road.


===Chinese===

两人接着聊普罗旺斯的古罗马遗迹，直到弗伦奇终于看了看表，说该走了。

他为自己的进展暗自得意。他相信，自己这趟登门的由头已经交代圆满，派克毫无所疑。他还弄到了足够的情由，足以查证这人从同斯坦利动身度假直到眼下的全部行踪。案子的尽头已经在望。他向自己许诺，今晚还要在这条路上再迈出一步。

## XVIII On Hampstead Heath / XVIII 汉普斯特德荒原

===Original===

French telephoned for a relief to take over the shadowing of Pyke during the afternoon, but by six o’clock he was back in Kepple Street, with the intention of being, if possible, present at the rendezvous of his new suspects.

===Chinese===

下午，弗伦奇打电话调了个替手来接替盯派克的梢，可到六点钟，他又回到了凯普尔街，打算尽可能亲眼盯着，看他这两名新嫌疑人会面。

===Original===

“He’s not shown up since,” Sergeant Harvey explained. “No one called, but one person left the house, a girl of about twenty. She returned in about two hours.”

===Chinese===

「打那以后他就没露过面，」哈维警士（Sergeant Harvey）汇报道。「没人登门，倒是有一个人出去过——一个二十岁上下的姑娘，两个钟头左右又回来了。」

===Original===

“Tall, good-looking girl, fair hair and blue eyes?” French suggested. “I know her﻿—the landlady’s daughter. I saw her when I called. That’ll do, Harvey. You can get away. I shall manage alone.”

===Chinese===

「高个子、模样俊俏、金发蓝眼的姑娘？」弗伦奇问道。「我认得她——房东太太的女儿。我上回来的时候就见过。行了，哈维，你可以下班了。我一个人应付得来。」

===Original===

It was a fine evening, but cold. The last glimmerings of daylight were disappearing from the sky and outside the radius of the street lamps stars began to show. It looked as if there would be frost later.

===Chinese===

这是个晴朗的傍晚，却很冷。天边最后一缕白昼的微光正在消隐，街灯光圈之外，星星开始露头。看样子夜里要上冻。

===Original===

To keep a secret watch on a house in a city street is a feat requiring no little skill. Though French had reached the stage in which he left most of such work to his underlings, he was a past master in the art. Yet as first seven, then eight, then nine struck from the surrounding clocks it required all his ingenuity to give the appearance of being detained on some ordinary and unimportant business. He strolled up and down, hiding in alley ways and behind projecting corners and moving on at intervals from one such place of vantage to another. He had engaged a taxi in case his quarry should do the same, and for a good part of the time he employed the old device of sitting back in its deepest recesses while the driver gave a lengthy first aid to his refractory engine.

===Chinese===

在城市街上对一座宅子秘密守望，是桩颇要本事的活计。弗伦奇虽早已到了把这类差事大多交给手下的地步，本人却依旧是此道老手。可是，随着四邻的钟先后敲过七点、八点、又敲过九点，他得绞尽脑汁，装出被什么寻常无关紧要的琐事绊住了身。他踱来踱去，一会儿藏进小巷，一会儿躲到凸出的墙角后头，不时又从一处这样的有利位置挪到另一处。他雇了一辆出租汽车，以防目标也来同一手；大半的时间里，他用的都是那套老法子——深深缩进车厢最里头，由着司机对那台不听使唤的引擎施以漫长的急救。

===Original===

At last, when he had become stiff with cold and was cursing mentally but steadily at the delay, Pyke appeared and started off quickly towards Russell Square. French drew back into the entry in which he was taking cover and allowed him to pass, then having signed to his driver to keep him in sight, he followed the quarry as closely as he dared. Pyke turned down Montague Street, walking by New Oxford Street and Charing Cross Road to Charing Cross Station. He seemed about to enter the station, but, suddenly glancing round, he turned to the right and dived down the steps to the Hampstead and Highgate tube. French had already settled up with his driver and he followed without delay.

===Chinese===

终于，就在他冻得浑身发僵、心里不声不响却一个劲儿咒骂这番耽搁的时候，派克露面了，快步朝罗素广场方向走去。弗伦奇缩回藏身的那条门道，放他过去，随后朝司机打了个眼色叫他盯住，便放开胆子尽量紧地跟了上去。派克拐进蒙塔古街（Montague Street），沿新牛津街和查令十字街走到查令十字车站。他看样子正要进站，却忽然回头扫了一眼，向右一转，一头扎下台阶，进了汉普斯特德与海格特地铁（Hampstead and Highgate tube）。弗伦奇早已和司机结清了账，当即跟了下去。

===Original===

Keeping close behind, French shadowed his man to the platform and entered the next coach of the Hampstead train which the other boarded. At each station he watched the alighting passengers, but it was not until Hampstead was reached that Pyke appeared. Twenty feet apart, the two men passed out into the street and up the hill towards the Heath.

===Chinese===

弗伦奇紧紧跟在后面，一路把人盯到站台，登上对方所上的那班开往汉普斯特德的列车，坐进隔壁一节车厢。每到一站，他都留意下车的旅客，可直到车抵汉普斯特德，派克才现了身。两人相隔二十英尺，先后出站上了街，顺坡朝荒原走去。

===Original===

At the wide space at the entrance to the Heath French fell further behind. Pyke was now strolling easily along, as if out for a breath of air before bed. He passed in towards the left near Jack Straw’s Castle, taking one of the side paths which led to the wilder areas. Here were fewer people, and French dropped back till he could just see the man’s light fawn coat like a faint smudge against the dark background of the trees.

===Chinese===

到了荒原入口处那片开阔地，弗伦奇坠得更远了。派克这时正信步闲逛，仿佛睡前出来透口气。他在杰克·斯特劳城堡（Jack Straw's Castle）附近折向左边，踏上一条通往更荒僻地带的侧径。这里行人更少，弗伦奇一路缀在后面，直到只望得见那人浅黄褐色的外套，像树丛深暗背景上的一抹淡淡影迹。

===Original===

Some four or five hundred yards from the entrance, down in the hollow, there are a number of thick clumps of bushes. Round one of these Pyke passed and instantly became invisible. French stopped in his turn and, tiptoeing to the nearer side of the clump, began stealthily to creep nearer. Though a certain amount of starlight showed in the open, among the bushes it was pitchy black. There was no wind, and in spite of the vast city surrounding the place, scarcely a sound broke the stillness. French crept on, stopping every few seconds to listen intently. Presently he heard movements close by. There were faint footsteps as if of a man pacing up and down, and occasional soft scraping of leaves and snapping of small twigs. Crawling under a bush, French crouched down and waited.

===Chinese===

离入口约莫四五百码，下去的洼地里，有一丛丛密实的灌木。派克绕到其中一丛后面，霎时没了踪影。弗伦奇也随之停步，踮着脚挪到灌木丛靠外的这一面，开始悄悄往近处爬。空地上虽透着几分星光，灌木丛间却黑得像泼了墨。四下无风，尽管周遭就是那座茫茫大城，寂静几乎不曾被一丝声响打破。弗伦奇匍匐向前，每隔几秒钟便停下凝神细听。不多时，他听见近旁有了动静。有轻轻的脚步声，像是一个人在来回踱步，间或夹杂着树叶的轻擦和细枝的脆断。弗伦奇爬到一丛灌木底下，蹲伏下来静候。

===Original===

For close on fifteen minutes the man paced backwards and forwards, while French grew stiff in his cramped position. Then light footsteps were heard on the path alongside the clump and a woman cleared her throat. The man moved out and said something in a low voice, to which the other replied. Then French heard Pyke say: “Come behind the shrubs. There is no one about and we shan’t be heard.”

===Chinese===

将近一刻钟，那人来回踱着，弗伦奇在蜷缩的姿势里渐渐僵了。随后，灌木丛旁的小径上响起轻巧的脚步声，一个女人清了清嗓子。那男人迎出去低声说了句什么，对方应了。接着弗伦奇听见派克说：「到灌木丛后头来。附近没有人，说话不会被听见的。」

===Original===

They moved close to French, though not so near as he would have liked. Listening intently, he could hear a good deal, though not all, of their conversation. The woman was Mrs. Berlyn and she was saying: “He suspects something; I’m sure of it. I’ve been just sick with terror all day. I thought I must see you, and this seemed the only way. I was afraid he might follow me if I met you publicly.”

===Chinese===

两人朝弗伦奇这边挪近了些，虽还不够近。他凝神细听，能听见他们谈话的大部分，虽非全部。那女人正是伯林太太，只听她说道：「他起了疑心，我敢肯定。我一整天都吓得要吐。我想非见你一面不可，看来也只有这个法子。我怕当众同你见面，他会跟了我来。」

===Original===

There was a murmur of Pyke’s voice, but French could not distinguish the words nor could he hear Mrs. Berlyn’s reply. Then he heard Pyke say: “I don’t think so. The note was there on the chimneypiece, but I’m quite certain it hadn’t been opened. I examined it carefully.”

===Chinese===

派克咕哝了一声，弗伦奇听不清字句，也听不见伯林太太的回答。随后他听见派克说：「我看不会。字条还在壁炉台上，可我敢肯定没被人拆过。我仔细查验过。」

===Original===

Mrs. Berlyn’s intonation sounded like a question.

===Chinese===

伯林太太的语调听上去像在发问。

===Original===

“Yes, I rang up Ganope and he said that no enquiries had been made,” Pyke rejoined. “It’s all right, Phyllis, I’m sure. Why, French explained that he had frightened you deliberately in order to find out your real opinion of Domlio.”

===Chinese===

「没错，我给加诺普挂了电话，他说没人来打听过，」派克答道。「没事的，菲莉斯，我敢担保。要知道，弗伦奇自己解释过，他是有意吓唬你，为的是探你对多姆利奥的真实看法。」

===Original===

They seemed to turn away, for during some moments French could only hear the murmur of their voices. Then apparently they approached again.

===Chinese===

两人像是转过身去了，有一阵子弗伦奇只听得见含混的话音。随后他们显然又凑近了。

===Original===

“… I thought it was Charles he suspected,” Mrs. Berlyn was saying; “then today I thought it was myself. He knows a lot, Jeff. He knows about Colonel Domlio’s letter and the photograph.”

===Chinese===

「……我原以为他怀疑的是查尔斯，」伯林太太正说着；「今天又以为是我。他知道得太多了，杰夫。他知道多姆利奥上校那封信的事，还有照片。」

===Original===

“Yes, but that’s all immaterial. He doesn’t know what really happened that night and he won’t know.”

===Chinese===

「知道是知道，可那些都无关紧要。那天夜里真正出了什么事，他不知道，也永远不会知道。」

===Original===

“But if he arrests us?”

===Chinese===

「可要是他把我们抓起来呢？」

===Original===

“Let him! As far as I am concerned, he can do nothing. He can’t break down the evidence of all those people in Manchester with whom I spent the night of the murder. You, I admit, haven’t such a watertight alibi, but it is impossible that he can prove you committed a crime of which you are absolutely innocent. And he can’t connect you with Charles. Remember that we’ve no reason to suppose he has the least idea that Charles is alive.”

===Chinese===

「让他抓好了！就我而言，他无计可施。案发那晚我在曼彻斯特，同我一起过夜的那些人，他扳不倒他们的证词。我承认，你的不在场证明没我这么滴水不漏，可他绝无可能证明你犯了一桩你全然无辜的罪。他也无法把你和查尔斯牵到一起。记住，我们没有半点理由认为他知道查尔斯还活着。」

===Original===

“If he finds that out, he’ll suspect Charles, and then he’ll suspect me as his accomplice.”

===Chinese===

「他要是查出来，就会疑心查尔斯，接着就会把我当作查尔斯的帮凶。」

===Original===

“I dare say,” Pyke admitted, “but he won’t find out. Poor old Charles! I said to him on that last day﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

「也难说，」派克承认道，「可他查不出来。可怜的老查尔斯！最后那一天，我对他说过……」

===Original===

The two moved off again, for Pyke’s voice died down into an unintelligible murmur. Again for some moments French could not distinguish what was said, then the words came more clearly.

===Chinese===

两人又走开了，派克的声音低下去，成了听不清的咕哝。有一阵子弗伦奇又辨不出说些什么，随后话语渐渐清晰起来。

===Original===

“No,” Pyke was saying. “I have a better plan than that. Tomorrow I’ll call at the Yard to see French and I’ll confess to the murder. I’ll say that my misery through remorse and suspense is worse than anything I could afterwards suffer, and that I just can’t bear it any longer. Lots of murderers have done that and he’ll suspect nothing. He’ll of course arrest me. Admittedly, he may arrest you also. This, of course, I should infinitely regret, but you will agree there is no other way. He’ll then think he has solved his problem and he’ll look no further afield. Before going to the Yard I’ll communicate with Charles and Charles can make his getaway. Then when Charles has had time to get out of the country I shall produce my Manchester witnesses and prove my innocence. The case against you will then break down.”

===Chinese===

「不，」派克正说着，「我有个比那更好的主意。明天我到苏格兰场去见弗伦奇，向他自首认下这桩谋杀。我就说，悔恨加悬心把我折磨得比日后无论受什么罪都难受，我实在熬不下去了。多少杀人犯都这么干过，他不会起半点疑心。他当然会逮捕我。诚然，他也可能连你一起逮捕。这我自然万分不愿，可你会同意，除此之外别无他法。到时候他就当自己的难题已经解开，再不会往别处去查。去苏格兰场之前，我先知会查尔斯，查尔斯便可以脱身。等查尔斯来得及逃出国境，我再搬出我在曼彻斯特的证人，证明我的清白。到那时，对你的指控也就不攻自破了。」

===Original===

“That’s all right except for two things,” Mrs. Berlyn returned. “If Charles believed he could get out of the country he would have done it long ago. Secondly, why, if you prove your innocence, will the case against me break down?”

===Chinese===

「这些都不错，只是有两点，」伯林太太回道。「第一，查尔斯若信得过自己出得了国，早就走了。第二，你既然洗清了自己，凭什么对我的指控也会跟着垮掉？」

===Original===

“Those are easily answered. By the time Charles was well enough to travel suspicion had been aroused and every policeman in the country was on the lookout for the Ashburton murderer. If I confess, the watch at the ports will be relaxed. Besides﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

「这两点都好回答。等查尔斯养到能够上路的时候，疑心早已兴起，全国的警察都在搜捕阿什伯顿的凶手。我若自首，各口岸的盘查就会松懈。再说……」

===Original===

Again the words became unintelligible. There was a faint sound of slowly pacing feet and the voice dwindled. But after a short time the footsteps again grew louder.

===Chinese===

话音又变得听不清了。缓缓踱步的脚响起一阵轻响，人声渐渐微弱。但过了不久，脚步声又近了。

===Original===

“… under the circumstances,” Mrs. Berlyn was saying, “but, of course, if Charles is taken we shall be accused of being his confederates.”

===Chinese===

「……在这样的处境里，」伯林太太正说着，「不过，查尔斯当然要是被捕了，我们就会被指控是他的同党。」

===Original===

“Very possibly. But, Phyllis, what can we do? We know old Charles is innocent, though things look so badly against him. We can’t let him down. We must do what we can to help him and take the risk.”

===Chinese===

「多半会。可是菲莉斯，我们又能怎么样呢？我们知道老查尔斯是无辜的，尽管情势对他那么不利。我们不能丢下他不管。我们必须尽力帮他，冒这个险。」

===Original===

“I know. I know. But isn’t the whole thing just *awful*! What have we done that we should get into such a position? It’s too much!”

===Chinese===

「我知道。我知道。可这一切岂不是*可怕*透了！我们做了什么，竟落到这般田地？太过分了！」

===Original===

Her voice, though carefully repressed, was full of suffering, and French could picture her wringing her hands and on the verge of tears. Pyke comforted her, though not at all in the tone of a lover, then went on:

===Chinese===

她的声音虽竭力压着，却满是痛苦，弗伦奇简直能想见她绞着双手、眼泪就要夺眶而出的样子。派克安慰了她，口吻却全然不像个情人，随后接着说道：

===Original===

“It’s time that we went back, old girl. Until I get the warning to Charles we mustn’t risk being seen together, therefore you’d better go on by yourself. I’ll follow in ten minutes.”

===Chinese===

「该回去了，老姑娘。在我把警讯递到查尔斯手里之前，我们不能冒险让人瞧见两人同行，所以你最好自己先走。我隔十分钟再跟上来。」

===Original===

They bade each other an agitated farewell and then Mrs. Berlyn’s light footsteps sounded on the path. For another quarter of an hour Pyke remained among the trees, his presence revealed by occasional movements and by the whistling under his breath of a melancholy little tune. Then at last he also moved away and French was able to stretch his aching limbs. Carefully he followed his man back to the tube station and eventually to his rooms in Kepple Street.

===Chinese===

两人心慌意乱地道了别，随后伯林太太轻巧的脚步声在小径上响起。派克又在树丛间待了一刻钟，偶有动静，还低声吹着一支忧郁的小调，显出他还在原处。最后他也走了，弗伦奇这才得以伸展一下酸痛的四肢。他小心跟着那人回到地铁车站，终于跟到凯普尔街他的寓所。

===Original===

He did not know what to make of the conversation to which he had just listened. The statements made were so surprising and unexpected that at first sight he was inclined to dismiss the whole thing as a blind, deliberately arranged to throw him off the scent. Then he saw that for several reasons this could not be. In the first place, Phyllis and Pyke did not know he was listening. In the second; such a plan would require careful prearrangement, and since his visit to Pyke the latter had had no opportunity of communicating with Phyllis Berlyn. Then there would be no object in such a scheme. They surely did not imagine that because of it French would relax his watch on them. Moreover, if it were false, its falseness would be demonstrated on the very next day. No, French felt the interview must be genuine.

===Chinese===

他不知该怎样理解方才这场谈话。那些话太惊人、太出人意料，乍看之下，他倾向于把整件事斥为障眼法——是蓄意布置来引他岔开线索的。可他随即看出，出于几点理由，这不可能。其一，菲莉斯和派克并不知道他在偷听。其二，这样的计策需要事先周密安排，而自他拜访派克以来，后者一直没机会与菲莉斯·伯林通气。再者，这样的骗局也毫无目的。他们总不至于指望弗伦奇会因此放松对他们的监视。何况，那话若是假的，转天就会不攻自破。不，弗伦奇认定这场会面必是真的。

===Original===

And if so, what a completely new view it gave of the crime! Berlyn, Phyllis, and Jefferson Pyke all apparently mixed up in the affair and all innocent! Who, then, could be guilty? French had to own himself completely puzzled. If this view were correct, the murderer must be someone whom he had not yet seriously considered.

===Chinese===

倘若如此，这桩罪案在他眼里便是何等全新的一幅图景！伯林、菲莉斯和杰斐逊·派克，显然全都卷进了这件事，却又全都无辜！那么，究竟谁可能有罪？弗伦奇不得不承认自己全然摸不着头脑。倘若这个看法不错，凶手必是他还没有认真考虑过的某个人。

===Original===

Unless it could be Domlio, after all. Nothing that the two had said directly precluded the possibility. Of course, in this case it was difficult to see why they should not denounce Domlio, if it would free themselves and Berlyn from suspicion. But then again, they might suspect Domlio, even perhaps be reasonably certain of his guilt, and yet unable to prove it.

===Chinese===

除非，说到底还是多姆利奥。两人说的话，没有一句直接排除这种可能。当然，若是这样，便难明白他们为何不去揭发多姆利奥，好让自己和伯林脱开嫌疑。可反过来说，他们也可能是疑心多姆利奥，甚至或许有相当的把握认定是他干的，只是拿不出证据。

===Original===

French continued to turn the matter over in his mind, and the more he did so the more he leaned to the opinion that Domlio must be, after all, the murderer. All the arguments which had before led him to this conclusion recurred to him with redoubled force and the difficulties in the theory seemed more and more easily surmountable. Domlio’s motor drive on the night of the crime, his denial of the trip, the hiding of the clothes and duplicator parts in the well, his depressed and absorbed manner﻿—these really were not accounted for by any theory other than that of the man’s guilt. And Domlio might easily have invented the story of the photograph and produced the letter to account for his nocturnal excursion.

===Chinese===

弗伦奇继续在心里翻来覆去盘算这件事，越盘算越倾向于认为，凶手毕竟还是多姆利奥。先前引他得出这个结论的种种理由，如今又加倍有力地浮上心头，而这套推想里的种种难点，似乎也越来越容易跨过。案发当夜多姆利奥驾车出行、他对那趟出行的否认、把衣服和复印机部件藏进井里、他那消沉出神的样子——除了认定此人有罪，任何别的推想都实在解释不通这些。至于照片那套说辞，多姆利奥编出来毫不费力，那封信更可以拿出来，给他的深夜出行作个交代。

===Original===

Puzzled and worried, French began to believe that he was on the wrong track in London and that he must return to Devon and try once more to get the truth from Domlio. But he would not relax his watch on Mrs. Berlyn and Pyke. Pyke he would himself shadow next day, so that if he communicated with Berlyn he, French, would learn the latter’s whereabouts.

===Chinese===

弗伦奇又困惑又焦灼，渐渐相信自己在伦敦这条线走岔了，必须回德文郡去，再设法从多姆利奥口中套出真相。但他不肯放松对伯林太太和派克的监视。第二天他要亲自盯派克的梢，这样，一旦派克同伯林接上了头，他弗伦奇就能知道后者的下落。

===Original===

He turned into the nearest telephone booth, and ringing up the Yard, arranged for reliefs for himself and Sergeant Carter. Then, Sergeant Deane having taken over the watch in Kepple Street, he went home.

===Chinese===

他拐进最近的电话亭，给苏格兰场挂了电话，为自己和卡特警士安排了换班。随后，迪恩警士（Sergeant Deane）接手了凯普尔街的监视，他便回家去了。

===Original===

Next morning he called at the Yard for Carter, and about nine o’clock the two men reached Kepple Street.

===Chinese===

第二天上午，他去苏格兰场接上卡特，九点前后，两人到了凯普尔街。

===Original===

“That’ll do, Deane. You may get away,” French greeted the night man. “Nothing stirred, I suppose?”

===Chinese===

「行了，迪恩。你可以下班了。」弗伦奇向值夜的同仁招呼道。「一夜没动静吧？」

===Original===

“Nothing, sir. No one in or out the whole night.”

===Chinese===

「没动静，先生。一整夜没人进出。」

===Original===

“Now, Carter,” French went on, “it’s your show mostly today. Pyke knows me and I’ll have to keep in the background. You stay about here and I’ll get a taxi and wait round the corner. If I see your signal I’ll come along.”

===Chinese===

「这样，卡特，」弗伦奇接着说，「今天主要看你的了。派克认得我，我只得退到暗处。你就在这一带守着，我去雇辆车，在拐角等着。一见你的信号我就过来。」

===Original===

Time soon began to drag for the watchers. Evidently their man was in no hurry. Ten o’clock came, then eleven, then eleven-thirty, and still he made no sign. French began to grow seriously uneasy.

===Chinese===

对守望的两人来说，时间很快开始磨人。他们的目标显然不着急。十点过了，十一点过了，十一点半也过了，他仍无动静。弗伦奇渐渐当真不安起来。

===Original===

At last he could stand it no longer. He got out of his taxi and strolled up to Carter.

===Chinese===

最后他实在按捺不住，下了车，踱到卡特跟前。

===Original===

“Go up to the door and ask for him, Carter. If he sees you say I sent you to ask if he would call round at the Yard any time this afternoon.”

===Chinese===

「卡特，你上门去找他。要是他看见你，就说是我派你来问一声，看他今天下午可否抽空到苏格兰场去一趟。」

===Original===

Taking his subordinate’s place, he watched him walk up to the door and knock. In a moment the door was opened and Carter disappeared.

===Chinese===

他接替了手下的位置，看着卡特走到门前叩门。不一会儿门开了，卡特一闪身进去了。

===Original===

For several minutes he remained inside, while French, growing more and more anxious every second, remained pacing impatiently up and down. Then Carter reappeared, and without any attempt at secrecy beckoned to French.

===Chinese===

卡特在屋里待了好几分钟，弗伦奇在外头一分一秒地越发焦灼，不耐烦地踱来踱去。随后卡特又露面了，也不避人耳目，径直朝弗伦奇招手。

===Original===

“What is it?” the latter whispered, sharply, as he joined the sergeant on the doorstep. “Anything wrong?”

===Chinese===

「怎么回事？」弗伦奇赶到台阶上与警士会合，压着嗓子厉声问。「出岔子了？」

===Original===

“I’m afraid so, sir. We can’t make him hear.”

===Chinese===

「恐怕是的，先生。我们怎么也叫不应他。」

===Original===

French swore. A wave of misgiving swept over him. Why in Heaven’s name hadn’t he arrested the man last night when he had the chance? He pushed into the house to meet an anxious-looking landlady in the hall.

===Chinese===

弗伦奇骂了一句。一阵不祥的预感兜头袭来。老天爷在上，昨晚分明有机会，他怎么就没把这人抓起来？他闯进宅子，在门厅里迎面撞上神色惶惶的房东太太。

===Original===

“I am Inspector French of Scotland Yard,” he explained, quickly. “Where is Mr. Pyke?”

===Chinese===

「我是苏格兰场的弗伦奇探长，」他急急说明道。「派克先生在哪儿？」

===Original===

“Lawks!” said the landlady, recognising her former visitor. “Are you police? And I thinking you were a friend of Mr. Pyke’s all the time.”

===Chinese===

「哎哟老天！」房东太太认出了先前的来客，叫道。「您是警察？我还一直当您是派克先生的朋友呢。」

===Original===

“Yes, madam, I am a police officer and I want to see Mr. Pyke at once. Where is he?”

===Chinese===

「是的，太太，我是警官，我要立刻见派克先生。他在哪儿？」

===Original===

The anxious look returned to the woman’s face.

===Chinese===

女人脸上的惶急之色又浮了上来。

===Original===

“He’s in his room,” she explained. “But he’s not had his breakfast and he won’t answer my knocking. He said last night that he had a chill and that he wouldn’t get up this morning, and for me not to disturb him. But that’s no reason why he shouldn’t answer a knock.”

===Chinese===

「他在自己房里，」她解释道。「可他早饭没吃，我敲门他也不应。昨儿夜里他说着了凉，今早不起床，叫我别去搅他。可总不能连敲门都不应啊。」

===Original===

“Which is his room?” said French, grimly. This should be a lesson to him to avoid his confounded trick of waiting till he was sure. If this man had slipped through his hands, any chance of that chief inspectorship was gone﻿—if his job itself remained.

===Chinese===

「他住哪一间？」弗伦奇沉声问道。这该给他一个教训，叫他改掉那个要命的毛病——总要等到十拿九稳才肯动手。这人若是从他指缝里溜了，那个总探长（chief inspector）的位子就甭想了——弄不好连饭碗都未必保得住。

===Original===

They went to a first-floor room at the back of the house and French knocked peremptorily. There was no reply.

===Chinese===

两人来到房子后部二楼的一间屋子，弗伦奇不容分说地敲了敲门。无人应答。

===Original===

“Down with the door, Carter. Put your shoulder to it with me.”

===Chinese===

「把门撞开，卡特。跟我一起用肩膀顶。」

===Original===

Despite the protests of the landlady, both men threw themselves on the door. With a tearing sound the screws of the keeper drew out and the door swung open.

===Chinese===

两人不顾房东太太的拦阻，一齐朝门撞去。随着一声撕响，锁座的螺钉被拔了出来，房门荡开了。

===Original===

The room was empty.

===Chinese===

房间是空的。

===Original===

It was a large room, comfortably furnished as a bed-sitting room. It was not disarranged in any way, but the lower sash of the window was fully up. French hurriedly crossed to it and looked out. It gave on the yard, and about four feet below the sill was the roof of a small shed. This shed ran along the side of the yard at right angles to the main house, its further gable being against the wall between the yard and the lane which passed at the back of the houses. The roof of the shed formed a direct passage from the room to the lane.

===Chinese===

那是间大屋子，作卧室兼起居室布置得颇为舒适。屋里陈设没有半点凌乱，只是窗子的下扇完全推了上去。弗伦奇急忙奔到窗前向外张望。窗子面对后院，窗台以下约四英尺处，是一间小棚屋的屋顶。棚屋沿着院子一侧搭起，与正房成直角，另一头的山墙抵着院墙——那道墙隔开后院与房后通过的小巷。棚屋的屋顶正好构成一条从房间直通小巷的过道。

===Original===

Though French had no doubt that Pyke had escaped by this route, he dropped out of the window and crawled over the roof tiles, searching for traces. These he speedily discovered. Someone had passed a short time previously.

===Chinese===

弗伦奇毫不怀疑派克走的就是这条路，但还是翻出窗口，爬过屋顶的瓦片搜寻痕迹。痕迹他很快就找到了：不久之前有人从此经过。

===Original===

His chagrin was too deep for words. Even the lurid phrase with which in times of stress he was wont to relieve his feelings now proved utterly inadequate. He was aghast at the extent to which he had been hoodwinked.

===Chinese===

他的懊恼非言语所能形容。平日情急之时，他惯用一句不堪入耳的粗话来发泄，此刻竟也全然不足以解气。自己被蒙骗到这个地步，他惊得目瞪口呆。

===Original===

For it was now evident that Pyke had known he had been shadowed to Hampstead Heath and that the whole conversation there had been designed to put him, French, off the track. Pyke was a cleverer man than he had thought. It seemed scarcely possible that the interview had been prearranged, and yet French could not imagine its being an improvisation. The whole thing was very puzzling.

===Chinese===

因为事情至此已经明白：派克早知道自己被跟到了汉普斯特德荒原，那边的那场谈话，整个儿就是设计来哄他弗伦奇岔开正路的。派克比他所想的更精明。要说那场会面是事先串通好的，似乎不大可能；可要说临场即兴，弗伦奇又无从想象。整件事扑朔迷离。

===Original===

However, at least one thing was clear. Jefferson Pyke and Mrs. Berlyn were the criminals for whom he was seeking. And he was on a hot scent. He would get Mrs. Berlyn at once, and Pyke with very little delay. The man had less than twelve hours’ start. He turned to Carter.

===Chinese===

不过，至少有一点是清楚的：杰斐逊·派克和伯林太太就是他要缉拿的罪犯。而且他正踩在热腾腾的线索上。他要立刻拿下伯林太太，派克也拖不了多久。这人上路还不足十二个钟头。他转向卡特。

===Original===

“Wait here for me, Carter,” he directed. “Have a look round and learn anything you can, but without disturbing things. I shall be back presently.”

===Chinese===

「卡特，在这儿等我，」他吩咐道。「四下里查看查看，能了解到什么算什么，只是别惊动现场的东西。我马上回来。」

===Original===

He ran out of the house, and calling up his taxi, was driven to Park Walk. There he soon spotted the man who was shadowing Mrs. Berlyn.

===Chinese===

他奔出宅子，唤来自己那辆车，直驶公园巷。很快，他就找到了盯伯林太太梢的那个人。

===Original===

“Anything to report, Jefferies?” he demanded, quickly. “She’s still there, all right?”

===Chinese===

「有情况要报吗，杰弗里斯？」他急急问道。「她还稳稳在屋里吧？」

===Original===

“Still there, Mr. French. No one left the house since I came on duty.”

===Chinese===

「还在，弗伦奇先生。我当班以来，没人离开过宅子。」

===Original===

“Good. Then come with me.”

===Chinese===

「好。那就跟我来。」

===Original===

He rang at the door and, when the servant opened, asked for Mrs. Berlyn.

===Chinese===

他按了门铃，女仆开门后，他便求见伯林太太。

===Original===

“She’s not up, sir,” the girl returned. “Last night she said she had a chill and did not want to be called or disturbed this morning. She said she probably wouldn’t want anything until lunch and not to bring up breakfast, as she was going to take a sleeping-draught and might not be awake.”

===Chinese===

「她还没起身呢，先生，」女仆回道。「昨儿夜里她说着了凉，今早不要人叫醒，也不要人搅扰。她说午饭前多半什么都不要，早饭也不必端上去，因为她要服一剂安眠药水，兴许还醒不过来。」

===Original===

Though still inadequate to relieve his feelings, French swore his lurid oath.

===Chinese===

弗伦奇又骂出那句不堪入耳的粗话，虽仍不足以解气。

===Original===

“Go and wake her now,” he ordered the scandalised girl. “Here, I’ll go to the door with you.”

===Chinese===

「这就去叫醒她，」他向那吓了一跳的女仆命令道。「来，我陪你到房门口。」

===Original===

The girl seemed about to object, but French’s tone overawed her. Hesitatingly she led the way.

===Chinese===

女仆像要抗辩，弗伦奇的语气却镇住了她。她迟疑着在前引路。

===Original===

“Knock,” said French. “Or wait. I will.” He gave a rousing knock at the door.

===Chinese===

「敲门，」弗伦奇说。「慢着，还是我来敲。」他抡起拳头，把门敲得山响。

===Original===

There was no answer.

===Chinese===

无人应门。

===Original===

“She, too, by thunder!” he growled, rattling the handle. The door was locked.

===Chinese===

「好啊，她也来这一手，见鬼！」他咆哮着，把门把手摇得咔咔响。门锁着。

===Original===

“Your shoulder, Jefferies!” and for the second time in half an hour French burst open the door of a bedroom. As in the former case, the room was empty.

===Chinese===

「上肩膀，杰弗里斯！」半小时之内，弗伦奇第二次撞开一间卧室的房门。和先前一样，房间是空的。

===Original===

But here there was no open window. Not only was the sash latched, but when French opened it and looked out he found that it was thirty feet above the pavement and that there was nothing to assist a descent.

===Chinese===

可这里没有敞开的窗子。窗扇不但扣着插销，弗伦奇推窗向外望去，还发现窗子离人行道足有三十英尺高，四下没有任何可以借力下攀的东西。

===Original===

A glance at the door explained the mystery. The key was missing. Evidently the occupant had left the room by the door, locking it behind her and removing the key. And the maid was able to supply the further information required. The girl, on coming down that morning, had found the yard door unlocked. Though she thought she had fastened it on the previous evening, she had supposed she must have overlooked it.

===Chinese===

看一眼门便解开了谜团。钥匙不见了。显然，屋里的人是从门出去的，出门时反锁上，还带走了钥匙。女仆又能补上所需的后一半情况：那天早晨她下楼时，发现后院的门没锁。她原以为自己头天晚上已经闩好，只当是自己疏忽了。

===Original===

French hurried down the narrow yard to the door which led out on the passage behind the houses, only to find that here again the door was locked and the key missing. Again and again he cursed himself for having underestimated the ability of these two people. Had he had the slightest idea that they had followed his progress so minutely, he would have employed very different methods. Even if he had not arrested them, he would have seen to it that they did not escape. The shadowing he had adopted would have been effective under normal conditions, but not where the victims were alive to their danger and ready to make a desperate bid for safety.

===Chinese===

弗伦奇匆匆穿过窄窄的后院，奔到通往房后过道的门前，却发现这里同样锁着，钥匙同样不见。他一遍又一遍咒骂自己低估了这两个人的本事。哪怕只稍微想到他们把自己的行动摸得如此精细，他也断然会用另一套办法。就算不逮捕他们，他也一定会看住，不叫他们跑掉。他采用的那套盯梢，在寻常情况下本行之有效，可遇上深知自身危险、横下心来拼死求逃的目标，就不管用了。

===Original===

But French was too sensible to cry over spilt milk. He had blundered. Very well, it could not be helped. What he had to do now was to retrieve his error, at the earliest moment possible. How could he most quickly get on the track of these criminals?

===Chinese===

不过弗伦奇是个明白人，不至于对着打翻的牛奶哭天抹泪。他失手了。好，失手已无可挽回。眼下要做的，是尽早把这一误挽回。怎样才能最快咬上这两个罪犯的踪迹？

===Original===

He returned to the house and made a rapid search for any clue that the lady might have left, but without result. Then leaving Jefferies in charge, he drove back to Kepple Street.

===Chinese===

他回到宅子里，匆匆搜了一遍，看那位太太可留下什么线索，却一无所获。随后他留下杰弗里斯守着，乘车赶回凯普尔街。

## XIX The Bitterness of Death / XIX 死亡的苦涩

===Original===

“Any luck, Carter?” French asked, quietly, as he reentered Pyke’s room.

===Chinese===

「有什么收获吗，卡特？」弗伦奇重新走进派克的房间，轻声问道。

===Original===

“No, sir. He has taken a suitcase with him, a brown leather one of medium size. I got the description from Mrs. Welsh. She says she noticed it here yesterday afternoon and it’s gone now. And he has taken all his outer clothes, his suits and overcoats and shoes, but most of his socks and underclothing are here in these drawers. I’ve been through everything, but I’ve not found anything useful.”

===Chinese===

「没有，长官。他随身带走了一只手提箱，棕色皮革的，中等大小——这是我从韦尔什太太（Mrs. Welsh）嘴里问出来的。她说昨天下午还在这儿见过，如今不见了。外衣他倒是全带走了——西装、大衣、皮鞋，可袜子和内衣裤大半还留在这些抽屉里。我把样样东西都翻遍了，没找出半点有用的东西。」

===Original===

“Let’s have a look.”

===Chinese===

「咱们来瞧瞧。」

===Original===

French hastily ran through the missing man’s effects. “Most of this stuff is foreign,” he observed, as he glanced over the clothes. “You see the Argentine marking on the collars and shirts. No, I don’t think there’s much to help us there. No books or papers?”

===Chinese===

弗伦奇匆匆翻检了一遍失踪者的衣物。「这些东西多半是外国货，」他扫了扫那些衣服说道。「你瞧，领子和衬衫上都是阿根廷的商标。不，我看这里头帮不上咱们什么忙。没有书或者文件吗？」

===Original===

“None, sir. But there’s a big heap of burnt paper in the grate.”

===Chinese===

「没有，长官。不过炉格里有一大堆烧过的纸。」

===Original===

“So I saw. We’ll go through it later on. Now ask the landlady to come here. Just sit down, Mrs. Welsh, will you? I want to know if you can tell me anything to help me to find your lodger. I’m sorry to say he is wanted on a very serious charge﻿—murder, in fact. Therefore, you will understand how necessary it is that you should tell me all you know.”

===Chinese===

「我看见了。那个回头再细查。现在请房东太太过来一趟。请坐，韦尔什太太。我想知道，您能不能告诉我些什么，帮我找到您的房客。说来遗憾，他正因一桩非常严重的罪名受到通缉——实际上，是谋杀。因此您会明白，把您知道的一切都告诉我，该有多么要紧。」

===Original===

Mrs. Welsh was thunderstruck, declaring again and again that she would not have believed it of so nice a gentleman. She was also terrified lest her rooms should suffer through the inevitable publicity. But she realised her duty and did her best to answer French’s questions.

===Chinese===

韦尔什太太惊得目瞪口呆，一迭声申辩，说这样一位和气的绅士，她说什么也不肯相信会做出这种事来。她又惶恐万分，生怕这桩案子免不了闹得满城风雨，连累她的房子没人来住。但她明白自己的本分，还是竭尽所能回答弗伦奇的问话。

===Original===

For a long time he gained no useful information, then at last an important point came out, though not in connection with his immediate objective.

===Chinese===

好半天，他都问不出什么有用的东西，最后总算冒出一条要紧的线索，虽说与他眼下的目标并不相干。

===Original===

Having given up for the moment the question of Pyke’s destination, French was casting around to see if he could learn anything connecting him with the crime, when he chanced to ask, had Mr. Pyke a typewriter?

===Chinese===

派克去了哪里，这个问题暂且搁过一旁，弗伦奇转而四下里探问，想查出些什么能把派克同那桩罪案联系起来。恰在此时，他随口问了一句：派克先生有打字机吗？

===Original===

“Not lately, he hadn’t,” Mrs. Welsh answered, “but he did have one for a time. I don’t know why he got it, for I never knew him to use it. But he had it there on the table for about three weeks.”

===Chinese===

「近来是没有，」韦尔什太太答道，「不过先前倒是有过一阵子。我不知道他弄它来做什么，从没见他使唤过。可它在那张桌子上摆了约莫三个星期。」

===Original===

“Oh!” said French, interested. “When was that?”

===Chinese===

「哦！」弗伦奇来了兴致。「那是什么时候的事？」

===Original===

“I couldn’t say exactly. Three months ago or more, I should think. But my daughter might remember. Vera is a typist and she was interested in the machine more than what I was. I’ll call her, if you like. She is at home on holidays.”

===Chinese===

「我说不准。总得三个月以前了吧，兴许更早。不过我女儿或许记得。薇拉（Vera）是个打字员，那台机器她比我上心。您要愿意，我就去叫她。她这几天休假在家。」

===Original===

Vera Welsh was the pretty girl with fair hair and blue eyes whom French had already seen. She smiled at him as she appeared in answer to her mother’s call.

===Chinese===

薇拉·韦尔什就是弗伦奇先前见过的那个俊俏姑娘，金发蓝眼。听见母亲唤她，她应声出来，朝他嫣然一笑。

===Original===

“We were talking of Mr. Pyke’s typewriter,” he explained. “Can you tell me what make it was?”

===Chinese===

「我们正说派克先生的打字机来着，」他解释道。「你能告诉我那是什么牌子的吗？」

===Original===

“Yes. I noticed it when I was dusting the room. It was a Corona Four.”

===Chinese===

「能。我掸灰的时候留意到的。那是台科罗纳四号（Corona Four）。」

===Original===

“And when did he get it?”

===Chinese===

「他是什么时候弄来的？」

===Original===

The girl hesitated. “Between three and four months ago,” she said at last, with a reserve which aroused French’s interest.

===Chinese===

姑娘迟疑起来。「三个月到四个月以前，」她终于答道；那份欲言又止，倒勾起了弗伦奇的兴趣。

===Original===

“Between three and four months?” he repeated. “How are you so sure of that? Was there anything to fix it in your mind?”

===Chinese===

「三个月到四个月以前？」他重复了一遍。「你怎么拿得这么稳？有什么事把它印在你脑子里了吗？」

===Original===

There was, but for some time the girl would not give details. Then at last the cause came out.

===Chinese===

确有其事，可姑娘好一会儿不肯细说。最后总算道出了缘由。

===Original===

It seemed that on the day after Vera had first noticed the machine she had had some extra typing to do in the office which would have necessitated her working late. But on that day her mother had not been well and she had particularly wanted to get home at her usual time. The thought of Mr. Pyke’s typewriter occurred to her, together with the fact that he had left that morning on one of his many visits to the country. She had thereupon decided to borrow the machine for the evening. She had brought her work home and had done it in his sitting room. She did not remember the actual day, but could find it from her records in the office.

===Chinese===

原来薇拉头一回注意到那台机器的第二天，公司里恰有些额外的打字活儿，得让她留到很晚。可偏偏那天她母亲身子不适，她一心想照平日的钟点回家。她想到了派克先生的打字机，又想起他那天早晨出了门，到乡下跑他那些数不清的差事中的一趟去了。于是她打定主意，把机器借来用一晚。她把活计带回家来，在他的客厅里做完了。具体是哪一天她记不真切，不过照公司里的记录可以查出来。

===Original===

“Very wise, if you ask me,” French said, sympathetically. “And what was the nature of the work you did?” He found it hard to keep the eagerness out of his voice.

===Chinese===

「要我说，这一着可聪明，」弗伦奇体恤地说。「那么你做的活儿，是什么性质的？」他发觉那股急切劲儿怎么也压不出自己的话音。

===Original===

“Just a copy of some tenders we had from America. I am in a hardware shop in Tottenham Court Road and it was about American lawnmowers and other gardening machines.”

===Chinese===

「不过是几份美国来件的报价单副本。我在托特纳姆法院路（Tottenham Court Road）一家五金行里做事，那批单子说的是美国造的割草机，还有别的些园艺机器。」

===Original===

“I understand. I suppose you don’t know what the copies were required for?”

===Chinese===

「明白了。这些副本要来做什么用，你大概不知道吧？」

===Original===

“Just for filing. The originals had to be sent away, and these were wanted for reference.”

===Chinese===

「就是归档。正本得寄出去，这几份留着备查。」

===Original===

French rose to his feet. Certainly the luck was not entirely against him.

===Chinese===

弗伦奇站起身来。看来运气并没有全与他作对。

===Original===

“Put your hat on, if you please, Miss Welsh, and come along with me to your office. I must see that copy. I can’t tell you how much you have helped me by telling me of it.”

===Chinese===

「那就麻烦你戴上帽子，韦尔什小姐，随我到你们公司去一趟。那份副本我非看不可。你把它说了出来，可真是帮了我天大的忙，我没法谢你。」

===Original===

The girl at first demurred, as she feared her employer might have views of his own as to the taking of important papers home from the office. But French assured her that he would see she did not suffer for her action. In fact, before she knew what was happening to her she was in a taxi on the way to the place.

===Chinese===

姑娘起初推托，生怕老板对把公司要紧文件带回家这种事，自有一套看法。但弗伦奇向她担保，决计不叫她因此吃亏。事实上，还没等她明白过来自己遭了什么，她已坐进出租汽车，往公司去了。

===Original===

On reaching the senior partner’s office, French was as good as his word. He explained the importance of his seeing the typescript, and saying that Miss Welsh had risked her job in the interests of justice, begged that the matter might not be held against her.

===Chinese===

一到大合伙人的办公室，弗伦奇便践行了诺言。他说明自己务必看那份打字稿一眼，事关重大；又说韦尔什小姐为了伸张正义，甘冒丢掉饭碗的风险，恳请不要把这一节记到她账上。

===Original===

Mr. Cooke shook his head over the incident, but, admitting to French that the girl was satisfactory, he agreed to overlook it. Then he rang for the papers in question.

===Chinese===

库克先生（Mr. Cooke）对这桩事直摇头，不过他向弗伦奇承认，姑娘人还算可靠，便答应不予计较。随后他按铃，叫人把那份文件取来。

===Original===

Ten seconds with his lens was enough for French. Here at last was the proof he had been looking for! The typing was that of the notes to the Veda works, to the Swansea garage, and to the magneto company.

===Chinese===

弗伦奇拿放大镜照了十秒钟，就够了。他苦苦寻觅的罪证，这下终于到手了！这份打字稿的字体，正是寄往维达工厂、斯旺西那家车库和磁电机公司那几张字条的字体。

===Original===

“I’m pretty glad to get this paper, Mr. Cooke,” he declared. “Now do you think you could let me keep it? Miss Welsh could perhaps type another copy for you?”

===Chinese===

「能拿到这张纸，我说不出有多高兴，库克先生，」他朗声道。「您看，能不能让我把它留下？韦尔什小姐或许可以再替您打一份？」

===Original===

Mr. Cooke was obliging, and in ten minutes the precious document was handed over. Stopping only to get the girl to certify on it that she had made it with Pyke’s machine, French hurried her away.

===Chinese===

库克先生很通融，十分钟后，这份珍贵的文件便交到了手上。弗伦奇只略停了停，让姑娘在文件上签注，证明它是用派克的机器打的，随后便带着她匆匆走了。

===Original===

“I’ll drive you home, Miss Welsh,” he said, with his pleasant smile. “You have been of the greatest help. Now I wonder if you could do something else for me,” and he began repeating the questions he had already put to her mother.

===Chinese===

「我开车送你回家，韦尔什小姐，」他带着和悦的笑容说。「你帮了大忙。不知你还能不能再替我做点别的？」说着，他把问过她母亲的那一套问题又问了一遍。

===Original===

Almost at once he got valuable information, though once again not on the matter immediately at issue.

===Chinese===

几乎立刻，他便得了条宝贵的线索，虽说这一回又与他当下追查的事无关。

===Original===

It appeared that on the previous afternoon Pyke had called the girl into his room and asked her if she would do a small commission for him. It was to take a letter to a lady in Chelsea. It concerned, so he said, an appointment to dine that evening, so that she would see that it was urgent. The matter was private and she was not to give the note to anyone but the lady herself, nor was she to mention it. To compensate her for her trouble and to cover the cost of taxis and so on he hoped she would accept a ten-shilling note. She had not thought this strange, as she knew him to be liberal in money matters. But she had wondered that a note about a dinner appointment should be so bulky. The envelope was of foolscap size and must have contained at least a dozen sheets. She had taken it to the address it bore and handed it to the lady﻿—Mrs. Berlyn at 70b Park Walk, Chelsea. She had not mentioned the matter to anyone.

===Chinese===

原来前一天下午，派克把姑娘叫进房间，问她可愿意替他办件小差使——送一封信给切尔西的一位太太。照他说，信上讲的是当晚的一个饭约，所以她看得出事情很急。这事关系隐私，字条只能交到那位太太本人手里，也不许对外人提起。为酬谢她的奔波，再贴补车马之类的开销，他希望她收下一张十先令的票子。她没觉得有什么蹊跷，因为她深知他在钱上一向大方。只是她纳闷，一封谈饭约的信怎么会这样厚墩墩的。那信封是大页纸（foolscap）的尺寸，里头起码装了一打信纸。她把信按封皮上的地址送到，交到了那位太太本人手里——住在切尔西公园巷 70b 号的伯林太太。这件事她对谁都不曾提起。

===Original===

Here was the explanation of the conversation French had overheard on Hampstead Heath. With a little thought he was able to follow the man’s mental processes.

===Chinese===

弗伦奇在汉普斯特德荒原上偷听到的那番对话，谜底原来在此。略一思索，他便把这人的心思理了个通透。

===Original===

In the first place, it was evident that Pyke had realised that he was suspected, as well as that French had opened Ganope’s note. He would guess, therefore, that French would shadow him continually until his meeting with Mrs. Berlyn, and would try to overhear what passed thereat. He would also see that for that very reason he was safe from arrest till the meeting had taken place, when this immunity would cease.

===Chinese===

首先，显而易见，派克已明白自己受了怀疑，也明白弗伦奇拆看过加诺普的字条。因此他不难猜到，弗伦奇会寸步不离地盯他的梢，直到他同伯林太太会面，还要设法偷听他们会面时谈些什么。他同样看得出，恰恰为了这个缘故，会面之前他稳保无被捕之虞，而这道护身符一到会面过后便告失效。

===Original===

But he wanted the night in which to escape. How could he stave off arrest until the following day?

===Chinese===

可他需要一夜的工夫好脱身。怎样才能把被捕拖到第二天呢？

===Original===

Clearly he had solved his problem by writing out the conversation, possibly with stage directions, as a playwright writes out the dialogue in his play. In it he had pledged himself to a visit to Berlyn on the morrow. If he could make French swallow the yarn he knew that arrest would be postponed in order that French might learn the junior partner’s whereabouts. He had then sent Mrs. Berlyn her “lines” and she had learnt them like any other actress. French ruefully admitted to himself that in spite of the absence of a rehearsal, the two had presented their little piece with astonishing conviction.

===Chinese===

显然，他的难题是这样解开的：把那场对话整个写出来，兴许还带着舞台提示，就像剧作家写戏中对白那样。在这场戏里，他许诺次日去访伯林。只要能叫弗伦奇把这套鬼话囫囵吞下，他便知道逮捕必定会往后拖，好让弗伦奇去摸那位小合伙人的下落。随后他把「台词」给伯林太太送去，她也像任何一个女演员似的，把台词背得烂熟。弗伦奇只得苦笑着自认：虽说不曾排演，这两位把那出小戏却演得惟妙惟肖，叫人深信不疑。

===Original===

On reaching the Yard, French’s first care was to set the great machine of the C.I.D. in operation against the fugitives. Among his notes he already had detailed descriptions of each, and he thought he would be safe in assuming that Pyke would wear his collar up and his hat pulled low over his eyes. Mrs. Welsh had described the suitcase, and burdened by this, French thought there was a reasonable chance of the man having been noticed.

===Chinese===

一回到苏格兰场，弗伦奇头一件事，便是开动 C.I.D. 那部庞大的机器来对付这两名逃犯。他的笔记里已各有两人详尽的相貌描述；他还觉得一条十拿九稳的是：派克准会竖着衣领、把帽檐压得低低的遮住眼睛。手提箱的样式韦尔什太太描述过了，弗伦奇想，提着这么个累赘，此人被人看在眼里的机会着实不小。

===Original===

A number of helpers were soon busy telephoning the descriptions to all the London police stations as well as to the ports. Copies were also sent for insertion in the next number of the *Police Gazette*. In a day or so all the police and detectives in the country would be on the lookout for the couple.

===Chinese===

不多时，一批帮手便忙着用电话把相貌特征通报伦敦各警局和各个港口。还寄出几份，预备登在下一期《警察公报》上。一两天内，全国的警察和侦探便都会留起神来，缉查这一对男女。

===Original===

With Mrs. Welsh’s help French made a list of the clothes likely to be in the suitcase. As these would have had a considerable weight, he thought it unlikely that Pyke would have walked very far. He therefore despatched three sets of men, one to make enquiries at the adjoining railways and tube stations, another to comb the neighbouring hotels and boardinghouses, and the third to search for a taxi driver who might have picked up such a fare in one of the nearby streets.

===Chinese===

弗伦奇借韦尔什太太之力，列了一张单子，开出手提箱里可能装着的衣物。这些衣物分量不轻，他想派克提着它们走不了多远。于是他派出三拨人：一拨去邻近的火车站和地铁站查询，一拨去细梳左近的旅馆和包膳公寓，第三拨去寻访可能在这些街上载过这样一位客人的出租汽车司机。

===Original===

It was not until these urgent matters had been dealt with that he turned to consider his second line of enquiry. Of Jefferson Pyke himself he knew practically nothing. What was the man’s history? Why was he remaining in England? Particularly, where had he been at the time of the crime and while the crate was at Swansea.

===Chinese===

这些急务料理停当，他才回过头来考虑第二条查访线。杰斐逊·派克其人，他几乎一无所知。这人是什么来历？为什么滞留英国？尤其是，案发当时、板条箱存置斯旺西的那几日，他人身在何处？

===Original===

He began operations by writing to the Lincoln police for all available information about Phyllis Considine, as well as Stanley and Jefferson Pyke. Then he sent a cable to the Argentine, asking the authorities there for details about Jefferson. He wired to the police at San Remo, Grasse, and in Paris, asking whether the cousins had stayed during the month of July at the various hotels Jefferson had mentioned. Lastly he rang up the Bibby Line offices to know if they could help him to trace two passengers named Pyke who had sailed from Liverpool to Marseilles on their *Flintshire* about four months previously.

===Chinese===

他先给林肯警方去信，调取有关菲莉斯·康西丁以及斯坦利·派克、杰斐逊·派克的一切可查资料。接着致电阿根廷，请那边的当局提供杰斐逊的详细情形。又给圣雷莫、格拉斯和巴黎的警方拍发电报，询问这对堂兄弟七月间可曾在杰斐逊提到过的那几家旅馆下榻。最后他打电话到比比轮船公司的办事处，问他们能否相助查一查：约莫四个月前，可有两位姓派克的乘客，搭乘他们的『弗林特郡号』从利物浦去了马赛。

===Original===

The Bibby people replied that the *Flintshire* had been home, but had left again for Rangoon. However if Mr. French would call at their office they would show him the passenger list and perhaps give him other required information.

===Chinese===

比比公司回话说，『弗林特郡号』曾回国一趟，可又已启航开往仰光去了。不过，弗伦奇先生若肯屈驾到他们办事处来，他们可以把旅客名单拿给他看，或许还能提供些别的所需情况。

===Original===

In an hour French was seated with the manager. There he inspected the list, which bore the names of Stanley and Jefferson Pyke, and he was assured that two gentlemen answering to these names had actually sailed.

===Chinese===

一个钟头后，弗伦奇已同经理坐在一起。他在那里查验了名单，上面果真有斯坦利·派克和杰斐逊·派克两个名字；对方向他保证，确有两位与这两个名字相符的先生搭船出航过。

===Original===

“If that is not sufficient for you, it happens that you can get further evidence,” the manager went on. “Mr. Hawkins, the purser of the *Flintshire*, broke his arm on the homeward trip. He went off on sick leave and if you care to go down to Ramsgate you can see him.”

===Chinese===

「倘若这还嫌不够，恰好还有进一步的凭据可寻，」经理接着说。「『弗林特郡号』的事务长霍金斯先生（Mr. Hawkins）在回程上摔断了胳膊，休病假下了船。您若肯跑一趟拉姆斯盖特（Ramsgate），便能见到他。」

===Original===

“I shall be only too glad,” French said.

===Chinese===

「那再好不过，」弗伦奇说。

===Original===

Armed with an introduction from the manager, French travelled down next morning to the Isle of Thanet. Mr. Hawkins was exceedingly polite and gave him all the information in his power. He remembered the Pykes having sailed on the last trip from Birkenhead to Marseilles. Stanley Pyke he had not come in contact with more than in the normal way of business, though they had once chatted for a few moments about the day’s run. But he had seen a good deal of Jefferson. He, Mr. Hawkins, had spent a year in the Argentine in Jefferson’s district and they had found they had many acquaintances in common. He had formed a high opinion of Jefferson, both as a man of the world and as a rancher. Both the cousins had seemed in every way normal, and several of the passengers had expressed regret when the two left the ship at Marseilles.

===Chinese===

翌日清晨，弗伦奇揣着经理的引荐信，南下萨尼特岛（Isle of Thanet）。霍金斯先生客气极了，把知道的情况尽数奉告。他记得派克兄弟俩搭的正是上一趟从伯肯黑德开往马赛的班船。斯坦利·派克，他不过是照常公务打过些交道，虽说有一回，两人曾就当天航程闲聊过几句。杰斐逊他倒是见得挺多。他霍金斯本人在阿根廷住过一年，正在杰斐逊那一带，两人发觉彼此熟识的人竟有不少。他对杰斐逊评价极高，不论作个见过世面的人，还是作个牧场主，都叫他佩服。堂兄弟俩看上去各方面都再正常不过，两人在马赛下船时，好几位乘客还直道可惜呢。

===Original===

On reaching London, French drove to the Houston. Showing his credentials, he asked whether two gentlemen, a Mr. Stanley and a Mr. Jefferson Pyke, had stayed there for one night towards the end of the previous July.

===Chinese===

回到伦敦，弗伦奇驱车前往休斯敦饭店。他出示证件，问去年七月下旬，可有两位绅士——一位斯坦利·派克先生、一位杰斐逊·派克先生——在那里歇过一夜。

===Original===

It was not to be expected that the reception clerk would remember either visitor. But she soon turned up the register. The names appeared on July 21st, both having been written by Stanley.

===Chinese===

指望接待员记得这两位客人，本就是奢求。不过她很快翻出了登记簿。两个名字登在 7 月 21 日那页上，都是斯坦利的手笔。

===Original===

“That’s scarcely good enough for me,” said French. “It doesn’t prove that they were both here.”

===Chinese===

「这对我来说可不太够，」弗伦奇说。「这证明不了他们俩都到过这儿。」

===Original===

“Practically it does,” the clerk returned. “You see, each was allotted a room. If the rooms had not been occupied the allocation would have been cancelled, for at that time we were turning people away every night. But we can soon settle it.” She looked up her account books. “Here,” she went on after a moment, “are the accounts in question. Mr. Stanley occupied Number Three Forty-six and Mr. Jefferson Number Three Fifty-one. The accounts were paid separately. I receipted Mr. Stanley’s, and Miss Hurst, another of our staff, receipted Mr. Jefferson’s. Curiously,” she went on, “I remember Mr. Stanley paying. He broke the basin in his room and we had some discussion as to whether he would be charged for it. He was, in the end.”

===Chinese===

「实际上证明得了，」接待员答道。「您想，两人各分了一间房。房间若没人住，分派的房号便会注销，因为那阵子我们天天夜里都在回客。不过这事马上就能弄清。」她翻出账簿。「喏，」过了一会儿她接着说，「这就是那两笔账。斯坦利先生住的是 346 号房，杰斐逊先生住的是 351 号。两笔账是分开付的。斯坦利先生那笔是我收的，杰斐逊先生那笔是我们另一位同事赫斯特小姐（Miss Hurst）收的。说来也巧，」她继续道，「斯坦利先生付账那回，我记得真真儿的。他把房里的洗脸盆打破了，为着要不要算他的账，我们还商议了一阵子。到头来还是算了。」

===Original===

This would have seemed ample confirmation of Jefferson’s statement to most people, but French, with his passion for thoroughness, decided to see the chambermaid. She remembered the incident and remembered also that the gentleman’s friend had occupied No. 351, as on her bringing his hot water in answering to his ring, he had said he was late and would she go and wake his friend in No. 351. She had done as requested, but the friend was already up.

===Chinese===

在多数人看来，这已足以坐实杰斐逊的供述，可弗伦奇抱着那股不彻查到底不罢休的癖性，还要见一见客房女侍。她记得打碎脸盆那桩事，也记得那位先生的朋友住的是 351 号房——因为有一回他打铃，她应铃送热水进去，他说自己起晚了，有劳她去叫醒 351 号房的朋友。她照办了，只是那位朋友早已起身。

===Original===

From the Houston, French walked to Kettle Street. Yes, Mr. Jefferson had taken the rooms on the 22nd July last, and though he had been frequently away for a day or two at a time, he had lived there ever since. Moreover, Mrs. Welsh’s records enabled her to say that he had been absent not only on the night of the crime, but also on the date when the crate was disposed of at Swansea.

===Chinese===

从休斯敦饭店出来，弗伦奇步行去凯普尔街。没错，杰斐逊先生去年 7 月 22 日租下了那几间房，此后虽时常出门一两天，却一直住在那里。况且，韦尔什太太的账簿使她能够断言：不但案发那晚他不在，板条箱在斯旺西脱手的那一天，他也不在。

===Original===

On his return to the Yard, French found that replies had come in from Paris and the Riviera. Stanley and Jefferson Pyke had stayed at the three hotels in question.

===Chinese===

弗伦奇回到苏格兰场，发现巴黎和里维埃拉的回电都已到了。斯坦利·派克和杰斐逊·派克，确曾在所说的那三家旅馆下榻。

===Original===

The next post brought a letter from the Lincoln police. It appeared that the three young people about whom enquiries had been made had lived in the district at the time mentioned. Dr. Considine was a well-known practitioner in the town until his death in 1912, but little was known of his daughter Phyllis save the mere fact of her existence. Stanley and Jefferson Pyke had lived with a relative in the suburbs and had attended a private school kept by a Dr. Oates. The relations between the girl and boys were not known, but it was probable that they had met, as they were in the same social set. More could probably be learned by further enquiries.

===Chinese===

下一班邮班送来了林肯警方的复信。据称，被查询的那三个年轻人，在所说的年月确曾住在该地区。康西丁医生生前是城里一位知名的开业医生，直至 1912 年去世；他的女儿菲莉斯，却除了确有其人这一点外，外界几乎一无所知。斯坦利·派克和杰斐逊·派克随一位亲戚住在城郊，上过一位奥茨医生（Dr. Oates）开办的私立学校。那姑娘同两个男孩之间有过来往与否，无人知晓，不过他们既同属一个社交圈子，多半是识得的。若再行查访，或许还能问出更多。

===Original===

This seemed to French sufficient to corroborate the statements of Jefferson Pyke and Mrs. Berlyn, and he advised the Lincoln police not to trouble further in the matter.

===Chinese===

弗伦奇觉得，这些已足以印证杰斐逊·派克和伯林太太的供词，便回复林肯警方，此事不必再劳神了。

===Original===

He had scarcely written his note when a cable from the Argentine police was handed to him. Jefferson Pyke was well known as the owner of an *estancia* in the Rosario district. He was believed to be comfortably off, though not wealthy. He answered the description given in the wire from Scotland Yard and had left for England on the boat mentioned.

===Chinese===

他那封复信才落笔，阿根廷警方的电报便递到手里。杰斐逊·派克在罗萨里奥（Rosario）地区拥有一座大牧场，是位知名人物。据信他家道殷实，虽算不得豪富。此人相貌与苏格兰场电文所述相符，并且已搭乘前述那班轮船动身赴英。

===Original===

With the exception of the fact that Jefferson was away from his rooms at the time of the crime, all this was disappointing to French. So far he had learned little to help in the building up of his case. On the contrary, the tendency was in Jefferson Pyke’s favour. He did not appear to be of the stuff of which murderers are made. Nor, from the purser’s description, did it seem likely that he was hatching a crime while on the *Flintshire*. On the other hand, there was no knowing what even the mildest man might do under the stress of passion.

===Chinese===

除了案发当时杰斐逊不在寓所这一条，这一切都叫弗伦奇丧气。至今为止，他查到的东西对构筑本案助益甚少。恰恰相反，情势倒渐渐偏向有利于杰斐逊·派克的一面。此人怎么看，都不像是杀人坯子。照事务长说来，他在『弗林特郡号』上也不像正酝酿着一桩罪案的样子。可话说回来，哪怕再温和的人，在情欲的重压之下会干出什么来，谁也说不准。

===Original===

But what really worried French was the fact that he had made no progress towards the tracing of his suspects’ present whereabouts. In vain he urged his men on to more intensive efforts. Nowhere could they learn anything to help.

===Chinese===

然而真正叫弗伦奇犯愁的是：两名嫌疑人的下落，他至今一寸也没摸着。他再三催促手下加紧查访，终归徒劳。哪儿也打听不出半点有用的消息。

===Original===

But he realised that there was nothing for it but patience. The business was necessarily slow, as it meant individual enquiries from everyone concerned. French did not dare to advertise, lest Pyke should see the notice and take still further precautions against discovery.

===Chinese===

但他也明白，眼下除了耐性，没有别的法子。这桩事注定快不起来，因为得挨个儿向每个相关的人查问。弗伦奇不敢登报声张，生怕派克见了告示，防备得愈发周密。

===Original===

The third day passed and the fourth, French growing more restless every hour. He now began to consider publicity﻿—broadcast descriptions, advertisements in the papers, even the offer of a reward for secret information. Finally he decided that if by the following evening no news had come in, he would put these agencies in operation.

===Chinese===

第三天过去了，第四天也过去了，弗伦奇一个钟头比一个钟头坐立不安。他开始盘算公开搜捕——广播相貌特征、报纸上登广告，甚至悬赏购求密报。最后他拿定主意：倘若到第二天入夜还没有消息传来，便把这些手段一一使出来。

===Original===

But the men of the C.I.D. are marvellously efficient and persistent. On returning from lunch on the fifth day, French learned, to his infinite satisfaction, that a taximan whose information might prove valuable had been found and was on his way to the Yard. Ten minutes later an intelligent-looking man in a driver’s uniform was shown in.

===Chinese===

不过，C.I.D. 的人马效率惊人，又有一股不挠的韧劲儿。第五天，弗伦奇吃过午饭回来，得知寻着了一个出租车司机，其情报说不定十分要紧，此刻正在来苏格兰场的路上——他心满意足，无以复加。十分钟后，一个身穿司机制服、模样精明的汉子被领了进来。

===Original===

“Good afternoon,” said French. “You have something to tell me? Just let me have your name and address and then go ahead.”

===Chinese===

「午安，」弗伦奇说。「你有情况要向我报告？请先报上姓名住址，然后就请讲吧。」

===Original===

William Service explained that he was the driver of a taxi in the employment of Metropolitan Transport, Ltd. On Monday night, the night in question, he had driven a fare to Euston for the 12:25 express. On leaving the station he was returning through Russell Square to his garage when, near the end of Kepple Street, he was hailed by a man from the sidewalk. He had not wished to take another fare, but the man had offered him an extra five shillings to do so and he had then agreed. The man was of medium height and build and dressed in a fawn coat and soft hat. Service could not describe his features, the brim of his hat being pulled low to meet his upturned collar. He was carrying a largeish suitcase.

===Chinese===

威廉·瑟维斯（William Service）说明，自己是大都会运输有限公司（Metropolitan Transport, Ltd.）雇用的一名出租车司机。星期一夜里，就是出事的那一夜，他送一位乘客去尤斯顿赶 12 点 25 分的快车。驶出车站后，他正取道罗素广场回车行去，走到凯普尔街街尾附近，被人行道上一个男人喊住。他本不想再拉活儿，可那人肯多出五先令，他便应承下来。那男人中等个子、中等身材，穿一件浅黄褐色外套，戴一顶软帽。瑟维斯形容不出他的相貌：帽檐压得低低的，正抵着竖起的衣领。他手里提着一只略大的手提箱。

===Original===

He desired Service to drive to The Boltons, which, the Inspector probably knew, was an oval with a church not far from Chelsea. (The Inspector knew it and recognised with delight that it was just beside Park Walk). There he was to pick up a lady and to drive them both to a house in Victory Place, not far from the Elephant and Castle. The lady had been waiting. As far as he could see, she answered the Inspector’s description. He had driven both parties to the address mentioned. It was a big house of working-class flats.

===Chinese===

他要瑟维斯把车开到博尔顿斯（The Boltons）去——那地方，探长想必知道，是切尔西不远处一片椭圆形的街区，当中有座教堂。（探长岂止知道；他还喜出望外地认出，那地方恰在公园巷旁边。）在那里，他要接上一位太太，再把两人一齐拉到胜利街（Victory Place）的一所房子去，离大象与城堡（Elephant and Castle）不远。那位太太早已等候在那里。就他看来，正与探长描述的相符。他把两位乘客送到了所说的地址。那是一座很大的工人公寓楼。

===Original===

“Good! You’ve told your story well,” French approved. “Now I want you to drive me to the place. I shall be ready in a moment.”

===Chinese===

「好！你这故事讲得清楚，」弗伦奇嘉许道。「现在我要你把车开到那地方去。我转眼就能动身。」

===Original===

The last lap! A kind of cold excitement took possession of French. It had been a long and troublesome case, but it was over now. Another fine feather in his cap; another step to that somewhat overdue chief-inspectorship for which he had been so long hoping! A few minutes, an hour at most, and the thing would be an accomplished fact.

===Chinese===

最后一程了！一种冷飕飕的兴奋攫住了弗伦奇。这桩案子又冗长又棘手，如今总算了结了。帽子上又添一支漂亮的翎毛；他盼望已久、已然姗姗来迟的总探长位子，又近了一步！几分钟，至多一个钟头，事情便成定局。

===Original===

Hastily calling his two assistants, Carter and Harvey, he set off with them for Victory Place. “It’s a big thing, this,” he explained. “There must be no mistake about it. If we let these people slip through our fingers we needn’t go back to the Yard.”

===Chinese===

他急急唤上两名助手卡特和哈维，带他们一同出发去胜利街。「这可是件大事，」他解释道。「万万出不得差错。这两人若是从咱们指缝里溜了，咱们也就不必回苏格兰场去了。」

===Original===

They drove to the end of the block containing the house, and, Carter and Harvey remaining in the taxi, French went alone to reconnoitre. He rang at a door in the basement and asked the woman who opened if she could direct him to the caretaker.

===Chinese===

车开到那所房子所在街段的尽头，卡特和哈维留在车里，弗伦奇独自前去踩探。他在地下室的门上打铃，问开门的女人，可否领他去见房屋管理员。

===Original===

“My husband’s the caretaker, sir,” she answered, “but he’s out at present. Is it anything I can do?”

===Chinese===

「当管理员的就是我男人，先生，」她答道，「只是这会儿出去了。有什么我能代办的吗？」

===Original===

“Probably you can,” said French, with his most winning smile. “I’m looking for the lady and gentleman who came in on last Monday night. They arrived by a late train and weren’t here till after midnight.”

===Chinese===

「多半办得到，」弗伦奇堆起他最动人的笑容说。「我在找上星期一夜里住进来的那对先生太太。他们搭的是夜班车，过了半夜才到。」

===Original===

“Oh yes. Mr. and Mrs. Perrin?”

===Chinese===

「哦，有的。佩林先生和佩林太太（Mr. and Mrs. Perrin）吗？」

===Original===

“That’s right. Which flat have they taken?”

===Chinese===

「正是。他们租的是哪一套？」

===Original===

“Number Nineteen. That’s the top one on the right of the stairs.”

===Chinese===

「十九号。楼梯右手顶头那一套。」

===Original===

“Thank you. Do you know if they are in at present?”

===Chinese===

「谢谢。他们这会儿在不在家，您可知道？」

===Original===

“Mrs. Perrin is out. I saw her go about half an hour ago. So far as I know, Mr. Perrin is in.”

===Chinese===

「佩林太太出去了。约莫半个钟头前，我亲眼见她走的。据我所知，佩林先生在家。」

===Original===

“Thanks. I’ll just go up and see.”

===Chinese===

「多谢。我这就上去看看。」

===Original===

French returned to the taxi.

===Chinese===

弗伦奇回到汽车旁。

===Original===

“The woman is out, but Pyke is supposed to be upstairs in Number Nineteen flat. You, Harvey, will stay in the entrance hall and watch the stairs and lift. Take him without fail if we miss him above. If the woman appears, don’t show up, but let her go in. Carter, you come upstairs with me.”

===Chinese===

「那女人出去了，不过派克想必在楼上十九号套房里。哈维，你守在门厅里，盯住楼梯和电梯。上面若放跑了他，你务必把他拿住。那女人若露面，你别现身，放她进去。卡特，你跟我上楼。」

===Original===

Harvey strolled to the door and became immersed in the list of flat-holders, while French and Carter began to climb the stairs. There were two flats on each storey, to right and left of the flights. When they reached the first landing French pointed to a fire-escape notice. They followed the pointing hand to the back of the house along a passage between the two flats, and, silently pushing open a door fitted with panic fastenings, saw an iron staircase leading down outside of the wall from the top storey to a paved yard.

===Chinese===

哈维踱到门口，埋头研究起住户名单来；弗伦奇和卡特则开始登楼。每层楼梯左右各有一套住房。到得第一个楼梯平台，弗伦奇指了指一张太平梯告示。两人顺着指处，穿过两套住房之间的过道来到楼后，悄没声儿推开一扇装有应急门闩的门，只见一道铁梯贴着墙外，自顶层直下到一方铺石板的小院。

===Original===

“You’ll have to stay and watch that, Carter. I can manage the blighter upstairs.”

===Chinese===

「你只好留下来守住这条路了，卡特。楼上那小子我一个人对付得了。」

===Original===

For a moment French wished he had brought another man. Then he thought of how many times he had carried out arrests single-handed. There was no difficulty. A whistle would bring his two men at top speed, and if by some incredibly unlikely accident he let Pyke slip through his hands, one or other of them would certainly take him on his way down.

===Chinese===

有那么一瞬，弗伦奇后悔没多带一个人来。转念一想，他孤身执行逮捕已不知多少回，从没什么为难处。一声唿哨，两个手下便会飞跑而至；就算出下不可思议的万一，真叫他放跑了派克，那家伙下楼时，也逃不出他们哪一个的手心。

===Original===

He silently mounted the stairs to the tenth storey. No. Nineteen was the top flat, but the stairs led on to a door on to the roof. French knocked at No. Nineteen. There was no answer. In a moment he knocked again, then after waiting a few seconds, he turned the handle.

===Chinese===

他悄声拾级而上，直登十层。十九号是顶层那套，楼梯却还往上通向一扇到屋顶去的门。弗伦奇敲了敲十九号的门。无人应声。片刻他又敲了一回，等了几秒钟，便拧动了门把手。

===Original===

The door was unlocked and French pushed it open and looked in. Through a tiny hall he could see into a living room, small and poorly furnished, and with a kitchenette in the rear. Other partially open doors from the hall led into bedrooms. So far as he could see, the place was deserted.

===Chinese===

门没有上锁，弗伦奇推门往里望去。穿过一方小小的门厅，能看见一间起居室，狭小而陈设寒素，后头带个小厨房。门厅旁几扇半开的门通向卧室。就他望见的而言，屋里空无一人。

===Original===
